### 5. **Test Patterns for Conditional Routing in Graph**

**From test_graph.py (lines 99-144):**

Graph uses a `should_continue` routing function:

```python
class TestShouldContinue:
    """Tests for the should_continue routing function."""

    def test_should_continue_no_error(self):
        """Returns 'continue' when state has no error."""
        state = _make_initial_state()
        assert should_continue(state) == "continue"

    def test_should_continue_with_error(self):
        """Returns 'error' when state has a fatal error."""
        state = _make_initial_state(error="Something went wrong")
        assert should_continue(state) == "error"
```

**Routing semantics (lines 127-144):**
- `error` field (singular) = fatal error → routes to "error" node
- `errors` field (plural) = accumulated warnings → does NOT block, continues processing

**Graph structure test (lines 43-59):**

def test_graph_has_six_user_nodes(self):
    """Compiled graph should have exactly 6 user-defined nodes."""
    from protocol_processor.graph import create_graph
    
    graph = create_graph()
    # LangGraph adds __start__ and __end__ internal nodes
    # User nodes are those without leading underscores
    all_nodes = graph.nodes
    user_nodes = {name for name in all_nodes if not name.startswith("__")}
    assert user_nodes == {
        "ingest", "extract", "parse", "ground", "persist", "structure",
    }

### 6. **Gemini Structured Output Pattern (for Reuse in Ordinal Resolution Agent)**

**The exact pattern used in `detect_logic_structure()` (structure_builder.py lines 55-145):**

```python
async def detect_logic_structure(
    criterion_text: str,
    field_mappings: list[dict[str, Any]],
) -> LogicDetectionResponse | None:
    """Detect AND/OR/NOT logic structure in criterion text using Gemini."""
    
    # 1. Guard conditions (skip if not needed)
    if len(field_mappings) <= 1:
        return None
    
    google_api_key = os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY")
    if not google_api_key:
        logger.warning("GOOGLE_API_KEY not set")
        return None
    
    try:
        # 2. Initialize LLM
        from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
        
        gemini_model_name = os.getenv("GEMINI_MODEL_NAME", "gemini-2.0-flash")
        gemini = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
            model=gemini_model_name,
            google_api_key=google_api_key,
        )
        
        # 3. Apply structured output constraint
        structured_llm = gemini.with_structured_output(LogicDetectionResponse)
        
        # 4. Build context-aware prompt
        mapping_lines = []
        for i, fm in enumerate(field_mappings):
            entity = fm.get("entity", "?")
            relation = fm.get("relation", "?")
            value = fm.get("value", "?")
            unit = fm.get("unit", "")
            unit_str = f" {unit}" if unit else ""
            mapping_lines.append(f"  [{i}] {entity} {relation} {value}{unit_str}")
        mappings_text = "\n".join(mapping_lines)
        
        prompt = (
            "You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Analyze the logical"
            " structure of this eligibility criterion.\n\n"
            f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n\n"
            f"Field mappings (indexed):\n{mappings_text}\n\n"
            "Instructions:\n"
            "- Determine how the field mappings are logically connected\n"
            "- Use AND when all conditions must be met simultaneously\n"
            "- Use OR when any one condition suffices\n"
            "- Use NOT to negate a condition\n"
            "- Use ATOMIC for leaf nodes that reference a single field_mapping by index\n"
            "- Return a tree where the root is AND, OR, NOT, or ATOMIC\n"
            "- Every ATOMIC node must have field_mapping_index set to a valid index"
            f" (0 to {len(field_mappings) - 1})\n"
            "- Each field_mapping index should appear exactly once\n"
        )
        
        # 5. Invoke and parse
        result = structured_llm.invoke(prompt)
        if isinstance(result, dict):
            response = LogicDetectionResponse.model_validate(result)
        else:
            response = result
        
        # 6. Validate response
        if not _validate_logic_tree(response.root, len(field_mappings)):
            logger.warning("Logic tree validation failed — invalid indices")
            return None
        
        return response
    
    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning("Logic detection failed: %s", e, exc_info=True)
        return None
```

**Key design patterns for reuse in ordinal resolution:**

1. **Graceful degradation**: Return `None` on any failure, let caller implement fallback logic
2. **Structured output schema**: Define a Pydantic model to match LLM output (here: `LogicDetectionResponse`)
3. **Validation**: Always validate response indices/references before returning
4. **Prompt engineering**: Provide indexed context about options, instruct LLM to use those indices
5. **Error handling**: Try-except at top level, log and return None rather than raising

### 8. **Data Models for Expression Trees**

**Pydantic schemas (from structure.py):**

```python
# LLM output schema (for Gemini structured output)
class LogicNode(BaseModel):
    node_type: str  # "AND", "OR", "NOT", or "ATOMIC"
    field_mapping_index: int | None  # Only for ATOMIC
    children: list[LogicNode] | None

class LogicDetectionResponse(BaseModel):
    root: LogicNode
    reasoning: str

# JSONB storage schema
class ExpressionNode(BaseModel):
    type: str  # "AND", "OR", "NOT", or "ATOMIC"
    atomic_criterion_id: str | None  # For ATOMIC nodes
    entity: str | None
    relation: str | None
    value: str | None
    unit: str | None
    omop_concept_id: str | None
    children: list[ExpressionNode] | None

class StructuredCriterionTree(BaseModel):
    root: ExpressionNode
    structure_confidence: str  # "llm" or "fallback"
    structure_model: str | None  # e.g., "gemini-2.0-flash"
```

## Detailed LangGraph Pipeline Architecture Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's everything you need to know to plan adding an "ordinal resolution" agent node:

---

### 1. GRAPH STRUCTURE & FLOW

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/graph.py`

**Current 6-node pipeline:**
```
START → ingest → extract → parse → ground → persist → structure → END
```

**Key characteristics:**
- **Conditional error routing** after ingest, extract, parse: `should_continue()` checks `state["error"]`, routing to END on fatal errors
- **Error accumulation pattern** for ground & structure: Both nodes skip fatal errors and continue processing, accumulating non-fatal failures in `state["errors"]` list
- **Always-proceed edges**: ground→persist and persist→structure never hit error END; they handle failures internally
- **Compilation**: Graph is compiled with optional checkpointer (AsyncPostgresSaver for production, None for tests)
- **Singleton pattern**: Graph and checkpointer are singletons, created once and reused

**To add ordinal resolution node**, you would:
1. Add `workflow.add_node("ordinal_resolution", ordinal_resolution_node)`
2. Add edge from previous node → ordinal_resolution
3. Add edge from ordinal_resolution → next node
4. Use `add_edge()` (always proceed) or `add_conditional_edges()` (error routing) depending on your error strategy

---

### 2. STATE DEFINITION

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/state.py`

**PipelineState TypedDict:**
```python
class PipelineState(TypedDict):
    # Input (always present)
    protocol_id: str
    file_uri: str
    title: str
    
    # Created by parse node
    batch_id: str | None
    
    # Processing artifacts (populated on-demand)
    pdf_bytes: bytes | None          # ingest → extract
    extraction_json: str | None       # extract → parse
    entities_json: str | None         # parse → ground
    grounded_entities_json: str | None  # ground → persist
    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict] | None  # re-extraction only
    
    # Output
    status: Literal["processing", "completed", "failed"]
    error: str | None                 # Fatal errors route to END
    errors: list[str]                 # Non-fatal accumulated errors
```

**Pattern for adding state fields:**
- **For intermediate data**: Use `| None` for optional fields (populated on-demand)
- **For JSON data**: Store as `str | None` to minimize token usage during state serialization (serialize/deserialize in node)
- **For errors**: Add to `state["errors"]` list for accumulation, not `state["error"]`

**For ordinal resolution**, you might add:
- `ordinal_resolutions_json: str | None` – JSON string of resolved ordinals (preserve token minimization pattern)

---

### 3. STRUCTURE NODE AS REFERENCE

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/structure.py`

**Pattern it follows:**
1. **Entry guard**: Check `state.get("error")` and return `{}` if fatal error (skip processing)
2. **Span tracing**: Use `pipeline_span()` context manager for observability
3. **Error accumulation**: Start with `accumulated_errors = list(state.get("errors") or [])`
4. **DB session pattern**: Use `with Session(engine) as session:` for database operations
5. **Parallel processing**: Uses `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` for concurrency control on LLM calls
6. **Helper function pattern**: Separate `_process_criterion()` for individual item processing
7. **Return value**: Always returns dict with `{"status": "completed", "errors": accumulated_errors}`
8. **AuditLog**: Records all decisions to DB (line 160-171)

**Key code structure:**
```python
async def structure_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    if state.get("error"):
        return {}
    
    protocol_id = state.get("protocol_id", "")
    with pipeline_span("structure_node", protocol_id=protocol_id) as span:
        try:
            accumulated_errors = list(state.get("errors") or [])
            with Session(engine) as session:
                # Query DB, process in parallel
                semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)
                tasks = [_process_item(...) for item in items]
                results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
                
                # Accumulate errors
                for error in results:
                    if error is not None:
                        accumulated_errors.append(error)
                
                # Log audit trail
                audit = AuditLog(...)
                session.add(audit)
                session.commit()
            
            return {"status": "completed", "errors": accumulated_errors}
        except Exception as e:
            # Return partial success if possible
            return {"status": "completed", "errors": accumulated_errors + [str(e)]}
```

---

### 4. GROUND NODE AS LLM AGENT REFERENCE

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py`

**LLM calling pattern:**
1. **Router pattern**: Load singleton router once (`_get_router()` creates TerminologyRouter on first call)
2. **Parallel entity processing**: `asyncio.gather(*tasks)` with semaphore(4) for concurrency
3. **Two-model architecture**: MedGemma for medical reasoning + Gemini for structured output
4. **Error isolation**: Return `(result, None)` on success, `(None, error_msg)` on failure to allow other entities to continue
5. **Agentic retry loop**: Max 3 attempts with reformulation if confidence < 0.5
6. **Audit trail**: Log all grounding decisions to AuditLog (line 522-530)
7. **Result accumulation**: Collect successful results and errors separately

**LLM call pattern (from medgemma_decider.py):**

    return EntityGroundingResult(...)

# ... process items ...
for (idx, entity), (result, error) in zip(items, outcomes):
    if result is not None:
        # Success path

return {"grounded_entities_json": json.dumps(results), "errors": accumulated_errors}

### 5. EXISTING AGENT PATTERNS

**MedGemma + Gemini two-model architecture:**

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/medgemma_decider.py`

**Key patterns:**

1. **Model initialization** (singleton):
   ```python
   def _get_medgemma_model() -> Any:
       global _model_loader
       if _model_loader is None:
           from inference.config import AgentConfig
           from inference.model_garden import create_model_loader
           _model_loader = create_model_loader(AgentConfig.from_env())

2. **Gemini structured output**:

       model=os.getenv("GEMINI_MODEL_NAME", "gemini-2.0-flash"),
       google_api_key=os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
   )

   result = structured_llm.invoke(prompt)
   ```

3. **Agentic reasoning loop** (max 3 attempts):
   - Attempt 1: Initial grounding via TerminologyRouter + MedGemma
   - Attempt 2-3: If confidence < 0.5, ask MedGemma 3 questions:
     - Q1: Is this valid medical criterion?
     - Q2: Is this derived to a standard concept?
     - Q3: Can this be rephrased for better search?
   - Gemini structures the answers and adds optional reformulation suggestions
   - If still failing after 3 attempts: route to `expert_review`

4. **Prompt templating** (Jinja2):
   ```python
   def _render_template(template_name: str, **kwargs) -> str:
       from jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader
       prompts_dir = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"
       env = Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(prompts_dir)))
       template = env.get_template(template_name)
       return template.render(**kwargs)
   ```

5. **API key pattern**:
   ```python
   google_api_key = os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY")
   if not google_api_key:
       raise ValueError("GOOGLE_API_KEY environment variable is required")
   ```

### 6. STRUCTURED OUTPUT PATTERNS

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/structure_builder.py`

**Logic detection with Gemini structured output:**
```python
async def detect_logic_structure(
    criterion_text: str,
    field_mappings: list[dict[str, Any]],
) -> LogicDetectionResponse | None:
    # Skip if single mapping (no logic to detect)
    if len(field_mappings) <= 1:
        return None
    
    # Use Gemini structured output
    gemini = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
        model=os.getenv("GEMINI_MODEL_NAME", "gemini-2.0-flash"),
        google_api_key=os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    )
    structured_llm = gemini.with_structured_output(LogicDetectionResponse)
    
    result = structured_llm.invoke(prompt)
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        response = LogicDetectionResponse.model_validate(result)
    else:
        response = result
    
    # Validate indices are in range
    if not _validate_logic_tree(response.root, len(field_mappings)):
        return None
    
    return response
```

**Pattern:**
1. Check preconditions (skip if not applicable)
2. Get API keys from environment
3. Initialize Gemini with `with_structured_output(YourSchema)`
4. Invoke with prompt
5. Validate response
6. Return None on any failure (triggers fallback in caller)
7. Fallback is always available for when LLM fails

---

### 7. ERROR HANDLING PATTERNS

**Fatal vs Non-Fatal errors:**

| Pattern | When | Behavior | Example |
|---------|------|----------|---------|
| **Fatal error** | Unrecoverable upstream failure | Set `state["error"]` → routes to END | Extract fails to parse PDF |
| **Non-fatal** | Individual item fails | Append to `state["errors"]` → continue processing | One entity fails grounding |
| **Error accumulation** | Multiple items may fail | Collect all failures, still return success | Structure node processes 100 criteria, 3 fail |

**Code pattern for non-fatal:**
```python
accumulated_errors = list(state.get("errors") or [])
try:
    # Process items
    for item in items:
        try:
            result = process(item)
        except Exception as e:
            accumulated_errors.append(str(e))  # Non-fatal
except Exception as e:
    # Fatal: return error dict
    return {"error": f"Fatal: {e}"}

return {"status": "completed", "errors": accumulated_errors}
```

---

### 8. DATABASE & YAML CONFIG PATTERNS

**Config file usage** (`config/routing.yaml`):
```yaml
routing_rules:
  Medication: [rxnorm, umls]
  Condition: [icd10, snomed, umls]
  Consent: {skip: true}
  Procedure: [snomed, icd10]
```

**Pattern for loading config:**
```python
def __init__(self, config_path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    self.config_path = config_path or _DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH
    with open(self.config_path) as f:
        self.config = yaml.safe_load(f)
```

**No automatic YAML updates from agent output** — configs are manually maintained. Agents produce structured results that get stored in DB (JSONB fields), not written back to YAML.

**DB operations pattern:**
```python
with Session(engine) as session:
    # Query
    stmt = select(Model).where(...)
    results = session.exec(stmt).all()
    
    # Create/update
    record = Model(...)
    session.add(record)
    
    # Batch operations
    session.flush()  # Get IDs without committing
    
    # Audit trail
    audit = AuditLog(...)
    session.add(audit)
    
    session.commit()
```

### 9. KEY DECISIONS FOR ORDINAL RESOLUTION NODE

Based on the architecture patterns, here's what you need to decide:

1. **Node placement**: After structure (has field_mappings) or between ground and structure?
2. **Error routing**:
   - Use `add_edge()` (like ground→persist) for error accumulation
   - Use `add_conditional_edges()` (like parse→ground) for fatal errors
3. **State fields**:
   - Read: `protocol_id`, `batch_id`, plus any state field with ordinal-related data
   - Write: Add `ordinal_resolutions_json: str | None` to TypedDict
4. **LLM strategy**:
   - Use MedGemma for medical reasoning about ordinal scales?
   - Use Gemini for structured output parsing?
   - Define Pydantic schema for ordinal resolution results
5. **Concurrency**: Use `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` for parallel LLM calls (standard pattern)
6. **Audit logging**: Always log decisions to AuditLog for full audit trail
7. **Fallback**: What happens when LLM fails? (Ground node: returns confidence=0.0; Structure node: wraps in AND)

async def ordinal_resolution_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Resolve ordinal scales in criteria.

        state: Current pipeline state.
        
    Returns:
        Dict with status and accumulated errors.
    """
    from protocol_processor.tracing import pipeline_span
    
    if state.get("error"):
        return {}
    
    protocol_id = state.get("protocol_id", "")
    with pipeline_span("ordinal_resolution_node", protocol_id=protocol_id) as span:
        try:
            batch_id = state.get("batch_id")
            if not batch_id:
                return {"status": "completed"}
            
            accumulated_errors = list(state.get("errors") or [])
            
            with Session(engine) as session:
                stmt = select(Criteria).where(Criteria.batch_id == batch_id)
                criteria = session.exec(stmt).all()
                
                semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)
                tasks = [_resolve_ordinal(c, protocol_id, session, semaphore) for c in criteria]
                results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
                
                resolutions = []
                for (resolution, error) in results:
                    if resolution:
                        resolutions.append(resolution)
                    elif error:
                        accumulated_errors.append(error)
                
                session.commit()
            
            return {
                "status": "completed",
                "errors": accumulated_errors,
                "ordinal_resolutions_json": json.dumps(resolutions),
            }
        except Exception as e:
            logger.exception(f"Ordinal resolution node failed: {e}")
            return {
                "status": "completed",
                "errors": (state.get("errors") or []) + [str(e)],
            }
```

---

### SUMMARY

The LangGraph pipeline uses a clear **flat, modular node architecture** with:
- **Conditional routing** for fatal errors (stops pipeline)
- **Error accumulation** for non-fatal failures (continues processing)
- **Singleton patterns** for reusable components (graph, router, models)
- **Two-model architecture** (MedGemma + Gemini) for medical reasoning + structured output
- **Parallel processing** with semaphores (concurrency control on LLM calls)
- **Full audit trail** (every decision logged to AuditLog)
- **Minimal state serialization** (complex data stored as JSON strings)
- **Always-available fallbacks** when LLM fails (graceful degradation)

To add ordinal resolution, follow the **structure_node** or **ground_node** pattern depending on whether you need conditional error routing or error accumulation.

     1→"""LangGraph workflow definition for the consolidated protocol processor.
     2→
     3→Defines a 6-node StateGraph for the unified protocol processing pipeline:
     4→START -> ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist -> structure -> END
     5→
     6→Error routing:
     7→- After ingest, extract, parse: conditional edges route to END on error
     8→- After ground: always proceeds to persist (ground uses error accumulation)
     9→- Persist handles partial/total failures internally
    10→- Structure uses error accumulation (same as ground)
    11→
    12→Per user decision (v2.0 Architecture): "Flat pipeline with expression tree phase"
    13→"""
    14→
    15→import os
    16→from typing import Any
    17→
    18→from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph
    19→
    20→from protocol_processor.nodes.extract import extract_node
    21→from protocol_processor.nodes.ground import ground_node
    22→from protocol_processor.nodes.ingest import ingest_node
    23→from protocol_processor.nodes.parse import parse_node
    24→from protocol_processor.nodes.persist import persist_node
    25→from protocol_processor.nodes.structure import structure_node
    26→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    27→
    28→
    29→def should_continue(state: PipelineState) -> str:
    30→    """Route to error END or continue to next node.
    31→
    32→    Args:
    33→        state: Current pipeline state.
    34→
    35→    Returns:
    36→        'error' if state has a fatal error, 'continue' otherwise.
    37→    """
    38→    return "error" if state.get("error") else "continue"
    39→
    40→
    41→def create_graph(checkpointer: Any = None) -> Any:
    42→    """Create and compile the 6-node protocol processing workflow graph.
    43→
    44→    The graph follows this flow:
    45→    START -> ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist -> structure -> END
    46→
    47→    Conditional error routing after ingest, extract, and parse.
    48→    Ground always proceeds to persist (ground uses error accumulation).
    49→    Structure uses error accumulation (same pattern as ground).
    50→
    51→    Args:
    52→        checkpointer: Optional LangGraph checkpointer for fault-tolerant execution.
    53→            When provided, state is persisted after each node, enabling retry-from-
    54→            checkpoint. Use PostgresSaver for production. Defaults to None (no
    55→            checkpointing).
    56→
    57→    Returns:
    58→        Compiled StateGraph ready for async execution.
    59→    """
    60→    workflow = StateGraph(PipelineState)
    61→
    62→    # Add all 6 nodes
    63→    workflow.add_node("ingest", ingest_node)
    64→    workflow.add_node("extract", extract_node)
    65→    workflow.add_node("parse", parse_node)
    66→    workflow.add_node("ground", ground_node)
    67→    workflow.add_node("persist", persist_node)
    68→    workflow.add_node("structure", structure_node)
    69→
    70→    # Entry point
    71→    workflow.add_edge(START, "ingest")
    72→
    73→    # Conditional error routing after ingest, extract, parse
    74→    for source, target in [
    75→        ("ingest", "extract"),
    76→        ("extract", "parse"),
    77→        ("parse", "ground"),
    78→    ]:
    79→        workflow.add_conditional_edges(
    80→            source,
    81→            should_continue,
    82→            {"continue": target, "error": END},
    83→        )
    84→
    85→    # Ground always proceeds to persist (error accumulation handles partials)
    86→    workflow.add_edge("ground", "persist")
    87→    # Persist proceeds to structure (expression tree building)
    88→    workflow.add_edge("persist", "structure")
    89→    workflow.add_edge("structure", END)
    90→
    91→    return workflow.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)
    92→
    93→
    94→# Singleton instances for reuse across outbox handler invocations.
    95→# Per Pitfall 1 from research: checkpointer is singleton, created once,
    96→# reused across all invocations. Do NOT create per-invocation.
    97→_graph = None
    98→_checkpointer = None
    99→# Context manager for PostgresSaver; kept entered for app lifetime.
   100→_checkpointer_cm: Any = None
   101→
   102→
   103→async def _get_checkpointer_async() -> Any:
   104→    """Get or create the AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer singleton.
   105→
   106→    Creates an AsyncPostgresSaver using DATABASE_URL from the environment.
   107→    Uses the async context manager and calls asetup() to ensure checkpoint
   108→    tables exist. The context is kept open for the process lifetime.
   109→
   110→    Returns:
   111→        AsyncPostgresSaver instance configured with the application database.
   112→
   113→    Raises:
   114→        KeyError: If DATABASE_URL environment variable is not set.
   115→    """
   116→    global _checkpointer, _checkpointer_cm  # noqa: PLW0603
   117→    if _checkpointer is None:
   118→        from langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.aio import AsyncPostgresSaver
   119→
   120→        db_url = os.environ["DATABASE_URL"]
   121→        _checkpointer_cm = AsyncPostgresSaver.from_conn_string(db_url)
   122→        _checkpointer = await _checkpointer_cm.__aenter__()
   123→        await _checkpointer.asetup()  # Create checkpoint tables if they don't exist
   124→    return _checkpointer
   125→
   126→
   127→async def get_graph() -> Any:
   128→    """Get or create the compiled graph singleton.
   129→
   130→    Compiles the graph with an AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer if DATABASE_URL
   131→    is available. Falls back to no checkpointer if DATABASE_URL is not set
   132→    (e.g. in unit tests).
   133→
   134→    Returns:
   135→        Compiled StateGraph instance.
   136→    """
   137→    global _graph  # noqa: PLW0603
   138→    if _graph is None:
   139→        try:
   140→            checkpointer = await _get_checkpointer_async()
   141→        except (KeyError, Exception):
   142→            # DATABASE_URL not set (e.g. unit tests) — compile without checkpointer
   143→            checkpointer = None
   144→        _graph = create_graph(checkpointer=checkpointer)
   145→    return _graph
   146→

     1→"""Pipeline state definition for the consolidated 5-node protocol processor.
     2→
     3→Minimal flat state using JSON strings for complex data to minimize token usage
     4→during LangGraph state serialization between nodes.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Literal
     8→
     9→from typing_extensions import TypedDict
    10→
    11→
    12→class PipelineState(TypedDict):
    13→    """Minimal state for the protocol processing pipeline.
    14→
    15→    Fields are populated on-demand as the pipeline progresses through nodes:
    16→    ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
    17→
    18→    Attributes:
    19→        protocol_id: UUID of the protocol being processed. Always present.
    20→        file_uri: GCS URI (gs://) or local path (local://) of the PDF.
    21→        title: Protocol title from upload metadata. Always present.
    22→        batch_id: UUID of the CriteriaBatch created by parse. None until then.
    23→        pdf_bytes: Raw PDF bytes. Populated by ingest, consumed by extract.
    24→        extraction_json: JSON string of extracted criteria from extract node.
    25→        entities_json: JSON string of entities for grounding from parse node.
    26→        grounded_entities_json: JSON string of grounded results from ground.
    27→        status: Current pipeline status. Updated by each node.
    28→        error: Fatal error message. Set on unrecoverable failure; routes to END.
    29→        errors: Accumulated non-fatal errors (e.g. individual grounding failures).
    30→            Partial failures are preserved alongside successes per user decision.
    31→    """
    32→
    33→    # Input (always present)
    34→    protocol_id: str
    35→    file_uri: str
    36→    title: str
    37→
    38→    # Created by parse node
    39→    batch_id: str | None
    40→
    41→    # Processing artifacts (populated on-demand)
    42→    pdf_bytes: bytes | None  # Populated by ingest, used by extract
    43→    extraction_json: str | None  # Populated by extract, used by parse
    44→    entities_json: str | None  # Populated by parse/ground
    45→    grounded_entities_json: str | None  # Populated by ground, used by persist
    46→
    47→    # Re-extraction context (optional, only present during re-extraction)
    48→    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict] | None
    49→
    50→    # Output
    51→    status: Literal["processing", "completed", "failed"]
    52→    error: str | None
    53→    errors: list[str]  # Accumulate partial failures per user decision
    54→

     1→"""Structure node: build expression trees from persisted criteria.
     2→
     3→Phase 2 pipeline node that runs after persist. Reads criteria from DB,
     4→detects AND/OR/NOT logic via Gemini, creates normalized atomic_criteria/
     5→composite_criteria records, and stores the expression tree JSONB.
     6→
     7→Error handling: same accumulation pattern as ground node. Individual
     8→criterion failures don't block others. Uses asyncio.Semaphore(4) for
     9→parallel LLM calls.
    10→"""
    11→
    12→from __future__ import annotations
    13→
    14→import asyncio
    15→import logging
    16→from typing import Any
    17→
    18→from api_service.storage import engine
    19→from shared.models import AuditLog, Criteria
    20→from sqlmodel import Session, select
    21→
    22→from protocol_processor.state import PipelineState
    23→from protocol_processor.tools.structure_builder import build_expression_tree
    24→
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→
    28→async def _process_criterion(
    29→    criterion: Criteria,
    30→    protocol_id: str,
    31→    session: Session,
    32→    semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,
    33→) -> str | None:
    34→    """Process a single criterion: build expression tree and store it.
    35→
    36→    Args:
    37→        criterion: Criteria record with non-empty field_mappings.
    38→        protocol_id: Protocol ID for the AtomicCriterion records.
    39→        session: Active SQLModel session.
    40→        semaphore: Concurrency limiter for LLM calls.
    41→
    42→    Returns:
    43→        None on success, error message string on failure.
    44→    """
    45→    async with semaphore:
    46→        try:
    47→            conditions = criterion.conditions or {}
    48→            field_mappings = conditions.get("field_mappings", [])
    49→
    50→            if not field_mappings:
    51→                return None
    52→
    53→            inclusion_exclusion = getattr(criterion, "criteria_type", "inclusion")
    54→
    55→            tree = await build_expression_tree(
    56→                criterion_text=criterion.text,
    57→                field_mappings=field_mappings,
    58→                criterion_id=criterion.id,
    59→                protocol_id=protocol_id,
    60→                inclusion_exclusion=inclusion_exclusion,
    61→                session=session,
    62→            )
    63→
    64→            criterion.structured_criterion = tree.model_dump()
    65→            session.add(criterion)
    66→
    67→            logger.info(
    68→                "Built expression tree for criterion %s (confidence=%s, atoms=%d)",
    69→                criterion.id[:12],
    70→                tree.structure_confidence,
    71→                len(field_mappings),
    72→            )
    73→            return None
    74→
    75→        except Exception as e:
    76→            error_msg = f"Structure build failed for criterion {criterion.id[:12]}: {e}"
    77→            logger.error(error_msg, exc_info=True)
    78→            return error_msg
    79→
    80→
    81→async def structure_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:
    82→    """Build expression trees for all criteria in the batch.
    83→
    84→    Reads persisted criteria from DB (written by persist node), detects
    85→    logic structure, creates normalized table records, and stores the
    86→    expression tree JSONB on each criterion.
    87→
    88→    Args:
    89→        state: Current pipeline state with batch_id and protocol_id.
    90→
    91→    Returns:
    92→        Dict with status='completed' and accumulated errors list.
    93→    """
    94→    from protocol_processor.tracing import pipeline_span
    95→
    96→    if state.get("error"):
    97→        return {}
    98→
    99→    protocol_id = state.get("protocol_id", "")
   100→    with pipeline_span("structure_node", protocol_id=protocol_id) as span:
   101→        span.set_inputs({"protocol_id": protocol_id})
   102→
   103→        try:
   104→            batch_id = state.get("batch_id")
   105→            if not batch_id:
   106→                logger.warning(
   107→                    "No batch_id in state for protocol %s — skipping structure",
   108→                    protocol_id,
   109→                )
   110→                span.set_outputs({"status": "completed", "note": "no batch_id"})
   111→                return {"status": "completed"}
   112→
   113→            accumulated_errors: list[str] = list(state.get("errors") or [])
   114→
   115→            with Session(engine) as session:
   116→                # Query criteria with non-empty field_mappings
   117→                stmt = select(Criteria).where(Criteria.batch_id == batch_id)
   118→                all_criteria = session.exec(stmt).all()
   119→
   120→                # Filter to criteria with field_mappings in conditions
   121→                qualifying = [
   122→                    c
   123→                    for c in all_criteria
   124→                    if c.conditions
   125→                    and isinstance(c.conditions, dict)
   126→                    and c.conditions.get("field_mappings")
   127→                ]
   128→
   129→                logger.info(
   130→                    "Structure node: %d/%d criteria qualify for"
   131→                    " expression tree building (protocol %s)",
   132→                    len(qualifying),
   133→                    len(all_criteria),
   134→                    protocol_id,
   135→                )
   136→
   137→                if not qualifying:
   138→                    span.set_outputs(
   139→                        {
   140→                            "status": "completed",
   141→                            "note": "no qualifying criteria",
   142→                        }
   143→                    )
   144→                    return {"status": "completed"}
   145→
   146→                # Process criteria with semaphore for LLM concurrency
   147→                semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)
   148→                tasks = [
   149→                    _process_criterion(c, protocol_id, session, semaphore)
   150→                    for c in qualifying
   151→                ]
   152→                results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
   153→
   154→                # Collect errors
   155→                for error in results:
   156→                    if error is not None:
   157→                        accumulated_errors.append(error)
   158→
   159→                # Write audit log
   160→                audit = AuditLog(
   161→                    event_type="structure_trees_built",
   162→                    actor_id="system:pipeline",
   163→                    target_type="criteriabatch",
   164→                    target_id=batch_id,
   165→                    details={
   166→                        "protocol_id": protocol_id,
   167→                        "criteria_processed": len(qualifying),
   168→                        "errors": len([e for e in results if e is not None]),
   169→                    },
   170→                )
   171→                session.add(audit)
   172→                session.commit()
   173→
   174→            logger.info(
   175→                "Structure node complete for protocol %s:"
   176→                " %d criteria processed, %d errors",
   177→                protocol_id,
   178→                len(qualifying),
   179→                len([e for e in results if e is not None]),
   180→            )
   181→
   182→            span.set_outputs(
   183→                {
   184→                    "criteria_processed": len(qualifying),
   185→                    "errors": len([e for e in results if e is not None]),
   186→                    "status": "completed",
   187→                }
   188→            )
   189→
   190→            return {
   191→                "status": "completed",
   192→                "errors": accumulated_errors,
   193→            }
   194→
   195→        except Exception as e:
   196→            logger.exception(
   197→                "Structure node failed for protocol %s: %s",
   198→                protocol_id,
   199→                e,
   200→            )
   201→            span.set_outputs({"error": str(e)})
   202→            return {
   203→                "status": "completed",
   204→                "errors": (state.get("errors") or []) + [f"Structure node failed: {e}"],
   205→            }
   206→

     1→# Phase 3b: Ordinal Scale Normalization with Agent-Assisted Concept Resolution
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→Phase 3 unit/value normalization is complete (157 tests). But ordinal scoring systems (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, Child-Pugh, GCS, APACHE, etc.) get `unit_concept_id = None` and `value_concept_id = None` because the normalizer is context-free and skips numeric values.
     6→
     7→## Design Philosophy: Lookup → Agent → Approve → Persist
     8→
     9→Instead of hardcoding all OMOP concept IDs upfront (which requires Athena verification), the system uses a three-tier resolution pattern:
    10→
    11→1. **Lookup**: Check YAML config for a previously-approved mapping → use it immediately
    12→2. **Agent**: If not found, an LLM agent proposes a mapping (SNOMED code → OMOP concept ID resolution)
    13→3. **Approve & Persist**: Human reviews the proposal, approves/modifies it, and it gets written back to the YAML for all future lookups
    14→
    15→This means the YAML starts with **structural knowledge** (entity aliases, LOINC codes, SNOMED codes — all verifiable from public sources) but OMOP concept IDs are populated incrementally through the agent/approval workflow.
    16→
    17→## Research Summary
    18→
    19→- `{score}` (OMOP 8527) is the standard UCUM unit for ordinal scales ([LOINC 89247-1](https://loinc.org/89247-1) uses `{score}` as its example unit)
    20→- ECOG: [LOINC 89247-1](https://loinc.org/89247-1), answer list [LL529-9](https://loinc.org/LL529-9), answer codes LA9622-7 through LA9627-6
    21→- SNOMED CT ECOG grades: 425389002 (grade 0) through 423409001 (grade 5) — verified via [BioPortal](https://bioportal.bioontology.org/ontologies/SNOMEDCT?p=classes&conceptid=424122007)
    22→- No new DB column needed — `unit_concept_id` + `value_concept_id` + `entity_concept_id` fully describe ordinal measurements per [OMOP CDM v5.4](https://ohdsi.github.io/CommonDataModel/cdm54.html)
    23→- Positive/negative results keep `unit_concept_id = NULL` per OMOP standard
    24→
    25→## Files to Modify
    26→
    27→| File | Change |
    28→|------|--------|

    61→      "1": { snomed_code: "422512005", loinc_answer: "LA9623-5", description: "Restricted" }

    66→

    76→    values:
    77→      "100": { description: "Normal, no complaints" }
    78→      "90":  { description: "Able to carry on normal activity" }
    79→      "80":  { description: "Normal activity with effort" }
    80→      "70":  { description: "Cares for self, unable to do active work" }
    81→      "60":  { description: "Requires occasional assistance" }

    85→      "20":  { description: "Very sick, active supportive treatment" }
    86→      "10":  { description: "Moribund" }

    88→
    89→  nyha:
    90→    entity_aliases:
    91→      - "NYHA"
    92→      - "NYHA class"
    93→      - "NYHA functional class"
    94→      - "New York Heart Association"
    95→      - "NYHA classification"
    96→    loinc_code: "88020-3"
    97→    unit_concept_id: 8527
    98→    values:
    99→      "1": { snomed_code: "420300004", description: "No limitation" }
   100→      "2": { snomed_code: "421704003", description: "Slight limitation" }
   101→      "3": { snomed_code: "420913000", description: "Marked limitation" }
   102→      "4": { snomed_code: "422293003", description: "Unable without discomfort" }
   103→```
   104→
   105→Adding new scales (Child-Pugh, GCS, APACHE, etc.) is a YAML-only change — no code modifications needed.
   106→
   107→## Step 2: `normalize_ordinal_value()` in `unit_normalizer.py`
   108→
   109→New `@lru_cache` loader + two public functions. Existing APIs untouched.
   110→
   111→```python
   112→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   113→def _load_ordinal_scales() -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[str, Any]]:
   114→    """Returns (alias_to_scale, scale_defs)."""
   115→```
   116→
   117→**Public function 1 — runtime normalization:**
   118→```python
   119→def normalize_ordinal_value(
   120→    value_text: str | None,
   121→    entity_text: str | None = None,
   122→) -> tuple[int | None, int | None] | None:
   123→    """Entity-context-aware ordinal value normalization.
   124→
   125→    Returns:
   126→        (value_concept_id, unit_concept_id) if entity matches an ordinal scale.
   127→        value_concept_id is the approved OMOP ID from YAML (or None if not yet resolved).
   128→        unit_concept_id is always 8527 ({score}) for ordinal scales.
   129→        Returns None (not a tuple) if entity doesn't match any ordinal scale.
   130→    """
   131→```
   132→
   133→Key behaviors:
   134→- Entity matching: exact alias first, then bidirectional substring containment (case-insensitive)
   135→- Value parsing: strips "grade"/"stage"/"class"/"score" prefixes, normalizes "2.0"→"2"
   136→- Returns `omop_value_concept_id` from YAML if present, `None` if not yet resolved
   137→- Always returns `unit_concept_id=8527` for recognized ordinal entities
   138→- Returns Python `None` (not a tuple) for non-ordinal entities → signals fall-through
   139→
   140→**Public function 2 — agent proposal utility:**
   141→```python
   142→def propose_ordinal_mappings() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   143→    """Scan ordinal_scales config and return entries missing omop_value_concept_id.
   144→
   145→    Returns a list of dicts, each with:
   146→      - scale: scale key (e.g., "ecog")
   147→      - grade: value string (e.g., "2")
   148→      - snomed_code: known SNOMED code (if any)
   149→      - loinc_answer: known LOINC answer code (if any)
   150→      - description: human-readable description
   151→      - status: "needs_resolution"
   152→
   153→    This output can be fed to an LLM agent or OMOP vocabulary lookup to propose
   154→    omop_value_concept_id values. Once approved, add them to the YAML.
   155→    """
   156→```
   157→
   158→This is a read-only utility — it identifies gaps. A future agent (or manual Athena lookup) fills them. Once an `omop_value_concept_id` is added to the YAML and approved, the normalizer uses it automatically on next cache refresh.
   159→
   160→## Step 3: Update `structure_builder.py` call site
   161→
   162→In `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`, add ordinal-first dispatch:
   163→
   164→```python
   165→from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import (
   166→    normalize_ordinal_value, normalize_unit, normalize_value,
   167→)
   168→
   169→# After _parse_value()...
   170→raw_unit = fm.get("unit")
   171→_, unit_concept_id = normalize_unit(raw_unit)
   172→
   173→value_concept_id: int | None = None
   174→entity_text = fm.get("entity")
   175→raw_value = str(fm.get("value", ""))
   176→
   177→# Ordinal-first: check if entity is an ordinal scale
   178→ordinal_result = normalize_ordinal_value(raw_value, entity_text)
   179→if ordinal_result is not None:
   180→    ordinal_value_cid, ordinal_unit_cid = ordinal_result
   181→    value_concept_id = ordinal_value_cid
   182→    if ordinal_unit_cid is not None:
   183→        unit_concept_id = ordinal_unit_cid
   184→elif value_text and value_numeric is None:
   185→    _, value_concept_id = normalize_value(value_text)
   186→```
   187→
   188→Result for ECOG <= 2: `value_numeric=2.0` (for comparison) + `unit_concept_id=8527` + `value_concept_id=<OMOP ID once approved, None until then>`.
   189→
   190→## Step 4: Tests
   191→
   192→**`test_unit_normalizer.py`** — Add `TestNormalizeOrdinalValue` (~18 tests):
   193→- Entity matching: exact alias, full name, case-insensitive, substring ("ECOG PS")
   194→- ECOG: all 6 grades recognized (unit_concept_id=8527 for each), grades are distinct where OMOP IDs exist
   195→- Edge cases: "2.0"→"2", "Grade 2" prefix strip, unknown grade 7 → `(None, 8527)`
   196→- Non-ordinal: "HbA1c" → `None`, None/empty entity → `None`
   197→- `{score}` unit: `normalize_unit("score")` → 8527
   198→- `propose_ordinal_mappings()`: returns entries missing OMOP IDs
   199→
   200→**`test_phase3_integration.py`** — Update existing + add:
   201→- ECOG criterion #11: `unit_concept_id` changes None → 8527
   202→- Coverage counts updated
   203→- New Karnofsky criterion: verify unit_concept_id=8527
   204→
   205→## Verification
   206→
   207→1. `uv run pytest tests/test_unit_normalizer.py -v` — 30 existing + ~18 new pass
   208→2. `uv run pytest tests/test_phase3_integration.py -v` — updated expectations pass
   209→3. `uv run pytest tests/ -v` — full suite green
   210→4. `uv run ruff check .` — clean
   211→5. `propose_ordinal_mappings()` returns a list of entries needing OMOP ID resolution
   212→6. After adding an OMOP ID to YAML → normalizer returns it on next invocation
   213→

Per the \"Lookup → Agent → Approve → Persist\" design, unknown ordinal entities should trigger an LLM agent node that proposes a mapping for human review.\n\n## Design\n\nAdd a 7th LangGraph node `ordinal_resolve` after `structure`:\n\n```\nSTART → ingest → extract → parse → ground → persist → structure → ordinal_resolve → END\n```\n\n### Flow\n\n1. **Identify candidates**: Query AtomicCriterion records from the batch where `unit_concept_id IS NULL`, `value_numeric IS NOT NULL`, and `unit_text IS NULL`. This combination (numeric value + no unit) is a strong signal for unrecognized ordinal scales. Categorical values (HIV status = \"positive\") are excluded because they have `value_text` not `value_numeric`.\n\n2. **Batch LLM call**: Send all candidate entities to Gemini in a single structured-output call. Prompt asks: \"For each entity, is this a clinical ordinal scoring system? If yes, propose YAML config.\" Uses Pydantic schema for structured output (same pattern as `detect_logic_structure()`).\n\n3. **Immediate update**: For entities the LLM confirms as ordinal, set `unit_concept_id=8527` on the AtomicCriterion record. This is safe — `{score}` is always the correct UCUM unit for ordinal scales.\n\n4. **Store proposals**: Write an AuditLog entry with `event_type=\"ordinal_scale_proposed\"` containing the full proposed YAML mapping (entity aliases, LOINC codes, value ranges). This feeds the human approval workflow.\n\n5. **Graceful degradation**: If `GOOGLE_API_KEY` is missing or the LLM fails, skip silently (same pattern as `detect_logic_structure()`). Records stay with `unit_concept_id=None` — no worse than before.\n\n## Files to Create/Modify\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `src/protocol_processor/schemas/ordinal.py` | **NEW** — Pydantic schemas for Gemini structured output |\n| `src/protocol_processor/tools/ordinal_resolver.py` | **NEW** — `resolve_ordinal_candidates()` LLM tool |\n| `src/protocol_processor/nodes/ordinal_resolve.py` | **NEW** — LangGraph node function |\n| `src/protocol_processor/state.py` | Add `ordinal_proposals_json: str | None` field |\n| `src/protocol_processor/graph.py` | Add node + edge: `structure → ordinal_resolve → END` |\n| `tests/test_ordinal_resolve.py` | **NEW** — Unit + E2E tests |\n| `tests/test_graph.py` | Update node count from 6 → 7 |\n\n## Step 1: Pydantic Schemas — `schemas/ordinal.py`\n\n```python\nclass OrdinalValueProposal(BaseModel):\n    grade: str                    # e.g. \"5\", \"A\", \"B\"\n    snomed_code: str | None = None\n    loinc_answer: str | None = None\n    description: str | None = None\n\nclass OrdinalScaleProposal(BaseModel):\n    entity_text: str              # original entity from AtomicCriterion\n    is_ordinal_scale: bool\n    confidence: float             # 0.0–1.0\n    scale_name: str | None = None # snake_case key, e.g. \"child_pugh\"\n    entity_aliases: list[str] = []\n    loinc_code: str | None = None\n    unit_concept_id: int = 8527   # always {score}\n    values: list[OrdinalValueProposal] = []\n    reasoning: str | None = None\n\nclass OrdinalResolutionResponse(BaseModel):\n    proposals: list[OrdinalScaleProposal]\n```\n\n## Step 2: LLM Tool — `tools/ordinal_resolver.py`\n\nFollow the exact `detect_logic_structure()` pattern from `structure_builder.py`:\n\n```python\nasync def resolve_ordinal_candidates(\n    candidates: list[dict[str, Any]],\n) -> OrdinalResolutionResponse | None:\n```\n\n- **Guard**: Return `None` if `GOOGLE_API_KEY` missing or candidates is empty\n- **LLM**: `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI` + `.with_structured_output(OrdinalResolutionResponse)`\n- **Prompt**: \"You are a clinical terminology expert. For each entity below, determine if it is a clinical ordinal scoring system...\" with indexed entity list + context (value, relation)\n- **Validation**: Filter proposals where `is_ordinal_scale=True` and `confidence >= 0.7`\n- **Return**: `None` on any failure\n\n## Step 3: Node Function — `nodes/ordinal_resolve.py`\n\nFollow the `structure_node` pattern exactly:\n\n```python\nasync def ordinal_resolve_node(state: PipelineState) -> dict[str, Any]:\n```\n\n1. Guard: return `{}` if `state.get(\"error\")` or no `batch_id`\n2. For confirmed ordinals: `UPDATE atomic.unit_concept_id = 8527`\n6. Write AuditLog with `event_type=\"ordinal_scale_proposed\"`, `details={\"proposals\": [...]}`\n7. field:\n```python\nordinal_proposals_json: str | None  # Populated by ordinal_resolve\n```\n\n**`graph.py`** — Add node and replace final edge:\n```python\nfrom protocol_processor.nodes.ordinal_resolve import ordinal_resolve_node\n\nworkflow.add_node(\"ordinal_resolve\", ordinal_resolve_node)\n\n# Replace: workflow.add_edge(\"structure\", END)\n#
